{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_13cc63f6e7f24cd5b3ab57d2","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Testing Isolation Patterns\n2\t\n3\tThis document describes the testing isolation patterns used across the NEEDLE codebase to prevent test contamination of production environments.\n4\t\n5\t## Overview\n6\t\n7\tNEEDLE tests often spawn real subprocesses (the `needle` binary itself) which can interact with bead stores, workspaces, and configuration. Without proper isolation, tests can:\n8\t\n9\t- Create phantom beads in real repositories\n10\t- Contaminate production bead stores\n11\t- Scan and modify real user workspaces\n12\t- Leave orphaned state files\n13\t\n14\t## The Three Pillars of Isolation\n15\t\n16\t### 1. HOME Directory Isolation (Required)\n17\t\n18\tAll tests that spawn the `needle` binary MUST isolate the HOME directory. The Explore strand (enabled by default) scans `workspace_root` (defaulting to `$HOME`) for bead workspaces.\n19\t\n20\t**Pattern: Set HOME in test environment**\n21\t\n22\t```rust\n23\tlet temp_home = TempHome::new()?;\n24\tcmd.env(\"HOME\", temp_home.path());\n25\t```\n26\t\n27\t**Why this matters:**\n28\t- The Explore strand auto-discovers workspaces under HOME\n29\t- Without isolation, test processes see real repos\n30\t- Phantom beads get created under test worker IDs\n31\t- Recovery requires manual bead cleanup or database repair\n32\t\n33\t### 2. Workspace/Tempdir Cleanup\n34\t\n35\tAll test-created directories MUST be cleaned up, even on panic. Use fixtures that implement Drop for automatic cleanup.\n36\t\n37\t**Pattern: RAII fixtures**\n38\t\n39\t```rust\n40\tlet temp_home = TempHome::new()?; // Auto-cleanup on drop\n41\tlet workspace = temp_home.create_workspace(\"test-ws\")?;\n42\t```\n43\t\n44\t**Implementation:**\n45\t- Use `tempfile::TempDir` for temporary directories\n46\t- Implement Drop for custom fixtures\n47\t- Never rely on test completion for cleanup\n48\t\n49\t### 3. Process Guarding\n50\t\n51\tLong-running subprocesses MUST be wrapped in guards that ensure cleanup.\n52\t\n53\t**Pattern: ProcessGuard**\n54\t\n55\t```rust\n56\tlet child = Command::new(\"needle\").spawn()?;\n57\tlet mut guard = ProcessGuard::new(child);\n58\t// Guard kills and waits on drop, preventing zombies\n59\t```\n60\t\n61\t**Why this matters:**\n62\t- Tests may panic before explicit cleanup\n63\t- Zombies accumulate and consume system resources\n64\t- Guards ensure cleanup in all code paths\n65\t\n66\t## Available Infrastructure\n67\t\n68\t### Fixtures in `tests/adapter_validation_tests.rs`\n69\t\n70\t- **`TempHome`**: Isolated HOME directory fixture\n71\t  - Creates standard subdirectories (`.cache`, `.config`)\n72\t  - Auto-cleanup on drop\n73\t  - Methods for creating workspaces and configs\n74\t\n75\t- **`TempWorkspace`**: Temporary workspace fixture\n76\t  - Creates directory structure\n77\t  - Initializes git repository\n78\t  - Creates bead store configuration\n79\t\n80\t- **`TempFile`**: Temporary file fixture\n81\t  - Creates files with custom content\n82\t  - Auto-cleanup on drop\n83\t  - Useful for test prompts and configs\n84\t\n85\t### Process Management in `tests/process_guard.rs`\n86\t\n87\t- **`ProcessGuard`**: Child process cleanup guard\n88\t  - Automatic kill and wait on drop\n89\t  - Explicit control methods\n90\t  - Prevents zombie processes\n91\t\n92\t### Workspace Management in `tests/workspace_fixtures.rs`\n93\t\n94\t- **Mock workspace structures**: For testing workspace discovery\n95\t- **Scenario builders**: For creating complex test scenarios\n96\t- **Mock bead stores**: For testing bead store interactions\n97\t\n98\t## When to Use Each Pattern\n99\t\n100\t| Test Type | HOME Isolation | Workspace Cleanup | Process Guard |\n101\t|-----------|----------------|-------------------|---------------|\n102\t| Unit tests (no subprocess) | Optional | Optional | Not needed |\n103\t| Integration tests (subprocess) | **Required** | **Required** | **Required** |\n104\t| Adapter validation | **Required** | **Required** | **Required** |\n105\t| Benchmark tests | Recommended | **Required** | **Required** |\n106\t| Property tests | **Required** | **Required** | Conditional |\n107\t\n108\t## Examples\n109\t\n110\t### Basic Adapter Validation Test\n111\t\n112\t```rust\n113\tuse adapter_validation_tests::*;\n114\t\n115\t#[test]\n116\tfn test_claude_adapter_config() {\n117\t    // 1. Isolate HOME\n118\t    let temp_home = TempHome::new().unwrap();\n119\t    \n120\t    // 2. Create test workspace\n121\t    let workspace = temp_home.create_workspace(\"test-ws\").unwrap();\n122\t    \n123\t    // 3. Validate adapter config\n124\t    let adapter_yaml = create_test_adapter(\"claude-sonnet\");\n125\t    let validation = validate_adapter_config(&adapter_yaml);\n126\t    \n127\t    assert!(validation.is_valid);\n128\t    // Auto-cleanup happens when fixtures drop\n129\t}\n130\t```\n131\t\n132\t### Integration Test with Subprocess\n133\t\n134\t```rust\n135\t#[test]\n136\tfn test_worker_with_real_process() {\n137\t    // 1. Isolate HOME\n138\t    let temp_home = TempHome::new().unwrap();\n139\t    \n140\t    // 2. Create workspace and bead store\n141\t    let workspace = temp_home.create_workspace(\"test-ws\").unwrap();\n142\t    workspace.create_bead_store().unwrap();\n143\t    \n144\t    // 3. Spawn needle process with isolated HOME\n145\t    let child = Command::new(\"needle\")\n146\t        .env(\"HOME\", temp_home.path())\n147\t        .arg(\"worker\")\n148\t        .arg(\"--once\")\n149\t        .spawn()\n150\t        .unwrap();\n151\t    \n152\t    // 4. Wrap in process guard\n153\t    let mut guard = ProcessGuard::new(child);\n154\t    \n155\t    // 5. Test logic\n156\t    let result = guard.wait().unwrap();\n157\t    assert!(result.success());\n158\t    \n159\t    // 6. Without proper isolation, tests can:\n\n- Create phantom beads in real repositories\n- Contaminate production bead stores\n- Scan and modify real user workspaces\n- Leave orphaned state files\n\n## The Three Pillars of Isolation\n\n### 1. HOME Directory Isolation (Required)\n\nAll tests that spawn the `needle` binary MUST isolate the HOME directory. The Explore strand (enabled by default) scans `workspace_root` (defaulting to `$HOME`) for bead workspaces.\n\n**Pattern: Set HOME in test environment**\n\n```rust\nlet temp_home = TempHome::new()?;\ncmd.env(\"HOME\", temp_home.path());\n```\n\n**Why this matters:**\n- The Explore strand auto-discovers workspaces under HOME\n- Without isolation, test processes see real repos\n- Phantom beads get created under test worker IDs\n- Recovery requires manual bead cleanup or database repair\n\n### 2. Workspace/Tempdir Cleanup\n\nAll test-created directories MUST be cleaned up, even on panic. Use fixtures that implement Drop for automatic cleanup.\n\n**Pattern: RAII fixtures**\n\n```rust\nlet temp_home = TempHome::new()?; // Auto-cleanup on drop\nlet workspace = temp_home.create_workspace(\"test-ws\")?;\n```\n\n**Implementation:**\n- Use `tempfile::TempDir` for temporary directories\n- Implement Drop for custom fixtures\n- Never rely on test completion for cleanup\n\n### 3. Process Guarding\n\nLong-running subprocesses MUST be wrapped in guards that ensure cleanup.\n\n**Pattern: ProcessGuard**\n\n```rust\nlet child = Command::new(\"needle\").spawn()?;\nlet mut guard = ProcessGuard::new(child);\n// Guard kills and waits on drop, preventing zombies\n```\n\n**Why this matters:**\n- Tests may panic before explicit cleanup\n- Zombies accumulate and consume system resources\n- Guards ensure cleanup in all code paths\n\n## Available Infrastructure\n\n### Fixtures in `tests/adapter_validation_tests.rs`\n\n- **`TempHome`**: Isolated HOME directory fixture\n  - Creates standard subdirectories (`.cache`, `.config`)\n  - Auto-cleanup on drop\n  - Methods for creating workspaces and configs\n\n- **`TempWorkspace`**: Temporary workspace fixture\n  - Creates directory structure\n  - Initializes git repository\n  - Creates bead store configuration\n\n- **`TempFile`**: Temporary file fixture\n  - Creates files with custom content\n  - Auto-cleanup on drop\n  - Useful for test prompts and configs\n\n### Process Management in `tests/process_guard.rs`\n\n- **`ProcessGuard`**: Child process cleanup guard\n  - Automatic kill and wait on drop\n  - Explicit control methods\n  - Prevents zombie processes\n\n### Workspace Management in `tests/workspace_fixtures.rs`\n\n- **Mock workspace structures**: For testing workspace discovery\n- **Scenario builders**: For creating complex test scenarios\n- **Mock bead stores**: For testing bead store interactions\n\n## When to Use Each Pattern\n\n| Test Type | HOME Isolation | Workspace Cleanup | Process Guard |\n|-----------|----------------|-------------------|---------------|\n| Unit tests (no subprocess) | Optional | Optional | Not needed |\n| Integration tests (subprocess) | **Required** | **Required** | **Required** |\n| Adapter validation | **Required** | **Required** | **Required** |\n| Benchmark tests | Recommended | **Required** | **Required** |\n| Property tests | **Required** | **Required** | Conditional |\n\n## Examples\n\n### Basic Adapter Validation Test\n\n```rust\nuse adapter_validation_tests::*;\n\n#[test]\nfn test_claude_adapter_config() {\n    // 1. Isolate HOME\n    let temp_home = TempHome::new().unwrap();\n    \n    // 2. Create test workspace\n    let workspace = temp_home.create_workspace(\"test-ws\").unwrap();\n    \n    // 3. Validate adapter config\n    let adapter_yaml = create_test_adapter(\"claude-sonnet\");\n    let validation = validate_adapter_config(&adapter_yaml);\n    \n    assert!(validation.is_valid);\n    // Auto-cleanup happens when fixtures drop\n}\n```\n\n### Integration Test with Subprocess\n\n```rust\n#[test]\nfn test_worker_with_real_process() {\n    // 1. Isolate HOME\n    let temp_home = TempHome::new().unwrap();\n    \n    // 2. Create workspace and bead store\n    let workspace = temp_home.create_workspace(\"test-ws\").unwrap();\n    workspace.create_bead_store().unwrap();\n    \n    // 3. Spawn needle process with isolated HOME\n    let child = Command::new(\"needle\")\n        .env(\"HOME\", temp_home.path())\n        .arg(\"worker\")\n        .arg(\"--once\")\n        .spawn()\n        .unwrap();\n    \n    // 4. Wrap in process guard\n    let mut guard = ProcessGuard::new(child);\n    \n    // 5. Test logic\n    let result = guard.wait().unwrap();\n    assert!(result.success());\n    \n    // 6. All cleanup happens automatically on drop\n}\n```\n\n## Testing Checklist\n\nBefore committing a new test:\n\n- [ ] Does the test spawn any `needle` subprocess? → **Must isolate HOME**\n- [ ] Does the test create temporary directories? → **Use fixture with Drop**\n- [ ] Does the test spawn long-running processes? → **Use ProcessGuard**\n- [ ] Does the test access real bead stores? → **Use mock store instead**\n- [ ] Does the test modify real repositories? → **Isolate to temp workspace**\n\n## Anti-Patterns to Avoid\n\n### ❌ Never Use Real HOME\n\n```rust\n// BAD: Test contaminates real environment\nlet home = env::var(\"HOME\").unwrap();\ncmd.env(\"HOME\", home);\n```\n\n### ❌ Never Skip Process Guarding\n\n```rust\n// BAD: Process becomes zombie if test panics\nlet child = Command::new(\"needle\").spawn()?;\n// Test logic...\nchild.wait()?;\n```\n\n### ❌ Never Rely on Manual Cleanup\n\n```rust\n// BAD: Cleanup never happens if test panics\nlet temp_dir = tempdir()?;\n// Test logic...\nfs::remove_dir_all(temp_dir)?;\n```\n\n## Historical Context\n\nThe importance of these patterns was established through several incidents:\n\n- **2026-07-20**: Non-isolated test created ~284 phantom beads across ~22 repos\n- **2026-08-05**: In-process test leaked into bead-forge store, truncated `.beads/issues.jsonl` to 0 bytes (2302 beads recovered)\n- **2026-08-09**: Duplicate bead claims from concurrent workers without proper serialization\n\nSee ADR-006, ADR-015, and the testing isolation policy in CLAUDE.md for full postmortems.\n\n## Further Reading\n\n- `tests/adapter_validation_tests.rs` - Comprehensive isolation fixtures\n- `tests/process_guard.rs` - Process cleanup implementation\n- `tests/workspace_fixtures.rs` - Workspace management utilities\n- `docs/adr/006-testing-isolation-policy.md` - ADR on testing isolation\n- `docs/adr/015-concurrent-same-repo-worker-isolation.md` - ADR on worker isolation\n","numLines":218,"startLine":1,"totalLines":218}}}

.output();\n500\t\n501\t    match result {\n502\t        Ok(output) => output.status.success(),\n503\t        Err(_) => false,\n504\t    }\n505\t}\n506\t\n507\t/// Get the version of an adapter Adapter validation test infrastructure.\n//!\n//! This module provides comprehensive test infrastructure for adapter validation,\n//! including isolated HOME setup helpers, workspace/tempdir cleanup fixtures, and\n//! documented isolation patterns for adapter testing.\n//!\n//! # Testing Isolation Pattern for Adapter Validation\n//!\n//! Adapter validation tests spawn real `needle` subprocesses via the adapter system,\n//! which means they can leak into the real user environment without proper isolation.\n//! This module enforces the following isolation pattern:\n//!\n//! ## 1. HOME Isolation (Required)\n//!\n//! All adapter validation tests MUST isolate the HOME directory to prevent:\n//! - Scanning of real user workspaces by the Explore strand\n//! - Contamination of production bead stores\n//! - Phantom bead creation in real repositories\n//!\n//! ### Pattern: Always Set HOME in Test Environment\n//!\n//! ```rust\n//! #[test]\n//! fn test_adapter_something() {\n//!     let temp_home = TempHome::new().unwrap();\n//!\n//!     // Isolate HOME for the spawned subprocess\n//!     cmd.env(\"HOME\", temp_home.path());\n//!\n//!     // Now the subprocess will only see test-controlled workspaces\n//! }\n//! ```\n//!\n//! ### Why This Matters\n//!\n//! The Explore strand (enabled by default) scans `workspace_root` (defaulting to `$HOME`)\n//! for bead workspaces. Without isolation, a test's spawned binary will:\n//! - Discover real repos under the test's worker ID\n//! - Create test beads in production stores\n//! - Leave orphaned state after test completion\n//!\n//! ## 2. Workspace/Tempdir Cleanup\n//!\n//! All test-created directories MUST be cleaned up, even if the test panics.\n//! This module provides [`TempHome`] and [`TempWorkspace`] fixtures that auto-cleanup\n//! on drop.\n//!\n//! ## 3. Process Guarding\n//!\n//! Long-running adapter processes MUST be wrapped in [`ProcessGuard`] to ensure\n//! cleanup even on test panic. See `tests/process_guard.rs` for the implementation.\n//!\n//! # Module Structure\n//!\n//! - **Isolation fixtures**: [`TempHome`], [`TempWorkspace`]\n//! - **Adapter helpers**: [`test_adapter_path`], [`create_test_adapter`]\n//! - **Validation utilities**: [`validate_adapter_config`], [`check_adapter_binary`]\n//! - **Process management**: Re-exports from `process_guard`\n//!\n//! # Example Usage\n//!\n//! ```rust\n//! use adapter_validation_tests::*;\n//!\n//! #[test]\n//! fn test_claude_adapter_config() {\n//!     let temp_home = TempHome::new().unwrap();\n//!     let adapter = create_test_adapter(\"claude-sonnet\");\n//!\n//!     let validation = validate_adapter_config(&adapter);\n//!     assert!(validation.is_ok());\n//! }\n//! ```\n\nuse anyhow::{Context, Result};\nuse std::fs;\nuse std::io::Write;\nuse std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\nuse tempfile::TempDir;\n\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Re-exports for convenience\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n// Note: ProcessGuard is available in the tests/process_guard.rs module\n// Tests that need process cleanup should use that module or implement their own guard\n\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Isolation Fixtures\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Temporary HOME directory fixture with automatic cleanup.\n///\n/// This fixture creates a temporary directory that can be used as HOME for\n/// adapter subprocess isolation. It automatically cleans up on drop.\n///\n/// # Purpose\n///\n/// Prevents adapter subprocesses from scanning real user workspaces and\n/// contaminating production bead stores.\n///\n/// # Example\n///\n/// ```rust\n/// let temp_home = TempHome::new()?;\n/// cmd.env(\"HOME\", temp_home.path());\n/// // subprocess now sees only test-controlled workspaces\n/// ```\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct TempHome {\n    /// The underlying temporary directory.\n    temp_dir: TempDir,\n    /// Path to the temporary HOME directory.\n    home_path: PathBuf,\n}\n\nimpl TempHome {\n    /// Create a new temporary HOME directory.\n    ///\n    /// # Returns\n    ///\n    /// A new `TempHome` fixture with an isolated temporary directory.\n    ///\n    /// # Errors\n    ///\n    /// Returns an error if the temporary directory cannot be created.\n    pub fn new() -> Result<Self> {\n        let temp_dir =\n            TempDir::new().context(\"Failed to create temporary directory for HOME isolation\")?;\n\n        let home_path = temp_dir.path().to_path_buf();\n\n        // Create standard HOME subdirectories\n        fs::create_dir_all(home_path.join(\".cache\"))\n            .context(\"Failed to create .cache directory in temp HOME\")?;\n        fs::create_dir_all(home_path.join(\".config\"))\n            .context(\"Failed to create .config directory in temp HOME\")?;\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            temp_dir,\n            home_path,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Get the path to the temporary HOME directory.\n    ///\n    /// # Returns\n    ///\n    /// A `PathBuf` pointing to the temporary HOME directory.\n    pub fn path(&self) -> &Path {\n        &self.home_path\n    }\n\n    /// Create a test workspace within this temporary HOME.\n    ///\n    /// # Arguments\n    ///\n    /// * `name` - The name of the workspace to create\n    ///\n    /// # Returns\n    ///\n    /// A `TempWorkspace` fixture contained within this HOME.\n    pub fn create_workspace(&self, name: &str) -> Result<TempWorkspace> {\n        TempWorkspace::new(self.path(), name)\n    }\n\n    /// Create a `.needle.yaml` configuration file in this HOME.\n    ///\n    /// # Arguments\n    ///\n    /// * `config` - The YAML configuration content to write\n    ///\n    /// # Returns\n    ///\n    /// The path to the created configuration file.\n    pub fn create_needle_config(&self, config: &str) -> Result<PathBuf> {\n        let config_path = self.path().join(\".needle.yaml\");\n        let mut file = fs::File::create(&config_path)\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to create .needle.yaml at {:?}\", config_path))?;\n        file.write_all(config.as_bytes())\n            .context(\"Failed to write .needle.yaml configuration\")?;\n        Ok(config_path)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Temporary workspace fixture with automatic cleanup.\n///\n/// This fixture creates a temporary workspace directory that can be used\n/// for adapter testing. It automatically cleans up on drop.\n///\n/// # Purpose\n///\n/// Provides isolated workspace directories for testing adapter behavior\n/// with different workspace configurations without affecting real repositories.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct TempWorkspace {\n    /// The underlying temporary directory (if owned by this fixture).\n    temp_dir: Option<TempDir>,\n    /// Path to the workspace directory.\n    workspace_path: PathBuf,\n}\n\nimpl TempWorkspace {\n    /// Create a new temporary workspace within a parent directory.\n    ///\n    /// # Arguments\n    ///\n    /// * `parent` - The parent directory (typically a TempHome)\n    /// * `name` - The name of the workspace to create\n    ///\n    /// # Returns\n    ///\n    /// A new `TempWorkspace` fixture.\n    pub fn new(parent: &Path, name: &str) -> Result<Self> {\n        let workspace_path = parent.join(name);\n\n        // Create the workspace directory\n        fs::create_dir_all(&workspace_path)\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to create workspace at {:?}\", workspace_path))?;\n\n        // Initialize as a git repository (many adapter operations require git)\n        let _output = std::process::Command::new(\"git\")\n            .arg(\"init\")\n            .current_dir(&workspace_path)\n            .output()\n            .context(\"Failed to initialize git repository in workspace\")?;\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            temp_dir: None, // We don't own a temp dir - we're inside a parent\n            workspace_path,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Create a standalone temporary workspace with its own temp dir.\n    ///\n    /// # Returns\n    ///\n    /// A new `TempWorkspace` fixture with its own temporary directory.\n    pub fn new_standalone() -> Result<Self> {\n        let temp_dir =\n            TempDir::new().context(\"Failed to create temporary directory for workspace\")?;\n\n        let workspace_path = temp_dir.path().join(\"workspace\");\n\n        fs::create_dir_all(&workspace_path)\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to create workspace at {:?}\", workspace_path))?;\n\n        // Initialize as a git repository\n        let _output = std::process::Command::new(\"git\")\n            .arg(\"init\")\n            .current_dir(&workspace_path)\n            .output()\n            .context(\"Failed to initialize git repository in workspace\")?;\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            temp_dir: Some(temp_dir),\n            workspace_path,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Get the path to the workspace directory.\n    pub fn path(&self) -> &Path {\n        &self.workspace_path\n    }\n\n    /// Create a `.beads/` directory structure in this workspace.\n    pub fn create_bead_store(&self) -> Result<PathBuf> {\n        let beads_dir = self.workspace_path.join(\".beads\");\n        fs::create_dir_all(&beads_dir)\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to create .beads directory at {:?}\", beads_dir))?;\n        Ok(beads_dir)\n    }\n\n    /// Create a bead-forge configuration file in this workspace.\n    pub fn create_bead_config(&self) -> Result<PathBuf> {\n        let beads_dir = self.create_bead_store()?;\n        let config_path = beads_dir.join(\"config.yaml\");\n\n        // Write a basic bead-forge configuration\n        let config_content = r#\"\n# Bead forge configuration for testing\nbackend: bead-forge\n\n# Test workspace configuration\nworkspaces:\n  - path: .\n\"#;\n\n        let mut file = fs::File::create(&config_path)\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to create bead config at {:?}\", config_path))?;\n        file.write_all(config_content.as_bytes())\n            .context(\"Failed to write bead configuration\")?;\n\n        Ok(config_path)\n    }\n}\n\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Adapter Helper Functions\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Get the path to a built-in adapter YAML file.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n///\n/// * `adapter_name` - The name of the adapter (e.g., \"claude-sonnet\")\n///\n/// # Returns\n///\n/// A `PathBuf` pointing to the adapter's YAML file, if it exists.\npub fn test_adapter_path(adapter_name: &str) -> Option<PathBuf> {\n    // Check both embedded and possible development locations\n    let possible_paths = vec![\n        // Embedded adapters (in the binary)\n        PathBuf::from(format!(\n            \"/usr/local/share/needle/adapters/{}.yaml\",\n            adapter_name\n        )),\n        // Development location\n        PathBuf::from(format!(\"../adapters/{}.yaml\", adapter_name)),\n        PathBuf::from(format!(\"adapters/{}.yaml\", adapter_name)),\n    ];\n\n    for path in possible_paths {\n        if path.exists() {\n            return Some(path);\n        }\n    }\n\n    None\n}\n\n/// Create a minimal test adapter configuration.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n///\n/// * `name` - The adapter name\n///\n/// # Returns\n///\n/// A YAML string representing a minimal adapter configuration.\npub fn create_test_adapter(name: &str) -> String {\n    format!(\n        r#\"\n# Test adapter: {name}\nname: {name}\ndescription: \"Test adapter for validation testing\"\nagent_cli: test-agent\nversion_command: \"test-agent --version\"\ninput_method: stdin\ninvoke_template: 'echo \"test prompt\" | test-agent --model {{{{model}}}}'\ntimeout_secs: 120\nidle_timeout_secs: 60\nhard_timeout_secs: 300\nprovider: test\nmodel: test-model\ntoken_extraction:\n  method: none\n\"#\n    )\n}\n\n/// Create a test adapter with custom configuration.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n///\n/// * `name` - The adapter name\n/// * `config_fn` - A function that modifies the base adapter configuration\n///\n/// # Returns\n///\n/// A YAML string representing the custom adapter configuration.\npub fn create_custom_adapter<F>(name: &str, config_fn: F) -> String\nwhere\n    F: FnOnce(&mut String),\n{\n    let mut config = create_test_adapter(name);\n    config_fn(&mut config);\n    config\n}\n\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Validation Utilities\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Validation result for adapter configuration.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct ValidationResult {\n    /// Whether the configuration is valid.\n    pub is_valid: bool,\n    /// List of validation errors (empty if valid).\n    pub errors: Vec<String>,\n    /// List of warnings (informational, not blocking).\n    pub warnings: Vec<String>,\n}\n\nimpl ValidationResult {\n    /// Create a successful validation result.\n    pub fn valid() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            is_valid: true,\n            errors: vec![],\n            warnings: vec![],\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a failed validation result.\n    pub fn invalid(errors: Vec<String>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            is_valid: false,\n            errors,\n            warnings: vec![],\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Add a warning to an existing validation result.\n    pub fn with_warning(mut self, warning: String) -> Self {\n        self.warnings.push(warning);\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Add multiple warnings to an existing validation result.\n    pub fn with_warnings(mut self, warnings: Vec<String>) -> Self {\n        self.warnings.extend(warnings);\n        self\n    }\n}\n\n/// Validate an adapter configuration YAML string.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n///\n/// * `adapter_yaml` - The adapter configuration YAML\n///\n/// # Returns\n///\n/// A `ValidationResult` indicating whether the configuration is valid.\npub fn validate_adapter_config(adapter_yaml: &str) -> ValidationResult {\n    let mut errors = vec![];\n    let mut warnings = vec![];\n\n    // Check for required fields\n    if !adapter_yaml.contains(\"name:\") {\n        errors.push(\"Missing required field: 'name'\".to_string());\n    }\n\n    if !adapter_yaml.contains(\"invoke_template:\") {\n        errors.push(\"Missing required field: 'invoke_template'\".to_string());\n    }\n\n    if !adapter_yaml.contains(\"input_method:\") {\n        errors.push(\"Missing required field: 'input_method'\".to_string());\n    }\n\n    // Validate timeout values if present\n    if let Some(timeout_caps) = extract_field_value(adapter_yaml, \"timeout_secs\") {\n        if let Ok(timeout) = timeout_caps.parse::<u64>() {\n            if timeout > 3600 {\n                warnings.push(format!(\n                    \"timeout_secs ({}) exceeds 1 hour - this may be unusually long\",\n                    timeout\n                ));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Validate input method value\n    if let Some(input_method) = extract_field_value(adapter_yaml, \"input_method\") {\n        match input_method.trim() {\n            \"stdin\" | \"file\" | \"arg\" => {}\n            other => {\n                errors.push(format!(\n                    \"Invalid input_method: '{}'. Must be one of: stdin, file, arg\",\n                    other\n                ));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if errors.is_empty() {\n        ValidationResult::valid().with_warnings(warnings)\n    } else {\n        ValidationResult::invalid(errors)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Check if an adapter binary exists on PATH.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n///\n/// * `binary_name` - The name of the binary to check\n///\n/// # Returns\n///\n/// `true` if the binary exists on PATH, `false` otherwise.\npub fn check_adapter_binary(binary_name: &str) -> bool {\n    let Shared ProcessGuard test utility for child process cleanup.\n//!\n//! This module provides a reusable `ProcessGuard` struct that ensures proper\n//! cleanup of child processes in integration tests, even if the test panics.\n//!\n//! # Example\n//!\n//! ```no_run\n//! use tests::process_guard::ProcessGuard;\n//! use std::process::Command;\n//!\n//! #[tokio::test]\n//! async fn test_with_process() {\n//!     let child = Command::new(\"sleep\")\n//!         .arg(\"10\")\n//!         .spawn()\n//!         .expect(\"failed to spawn process\");\n//!\n//!     let mut guard = ProcessGuard::new(child);\n//!\n//!     // Test logic here...\n//!\n//!     // ProcessGuard ensures cleanup on drop, even if test panics\n//!     let _ = guard.wait();\n//! }\n//! ```\n\nuse std::io;\nuse std::process::{Child, ExitStatus};\n\n/// Guard for a child process that ensures cleanup on drop.\n///\n/// `ProcessGuard` wraps a `std::process::Child` and provides:\n/// - Automatic cleanup via `Drop` implementation (kills and waits to prevent zombies)\n/// - Interface methods (`wait()`, `kill()`, `try_wait()`) for explicit control\n/// - Safe ownership transfer to prevent double-wait issues\n///\n/// # Purpose\n///\n/// In integration tests that spawn long-running worker processes, we need to ensure:\n/// 1. The process is cleaned up even if the test panics\n/// 2. Zombies are prevented (killed processes must be waited on)\n/// 3. Tests have explicit control over process lifecycle\n///\n/// # Implementation\n///\n/// The guard wraps `Option<Child>` to enable safe ownership transfer in `Drop`.\n/// When dropped, it kills the process and waits for the exit status to prevent zombies.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct ProcessGuard {\n    inner: Option<Child>,\n}\n\nimpl ProcessGuard {\n    /// Create a new ProcessGuard from a child process.\n    ///\n    /// # Arguments\n    ///\n    /// * `child` - The child process to guard\n    ///\n    /// # Returns\n    ///\n    /// Returns a `ProcessGuard` that takes ownership of the child process.\n    ///\n    /// # Example\n    ///\n    /// ```no_run\n    /// # use std::process::Command;\n    /// # use tests::process_guard::ProcessGuard;\n    /// let child = Command::new(\"sleep\").arg(\"10\").spawn().unwrap();\n    /// let guard = ProcessGuard::new(child);\n    /// ```\n    pub fn new(child: Child) -> Self {\n        Self { inner: Some(child) }\n    }\n\n    /// Try to wait for the process without blocking.\n    ///\n    /// This method checks if the process has exited without blocking the thread.\n    ///\n    /// # Returns\n    ///\n    /// - `Ok(Some(status))` - Process has exited with the given status\n    /// - `Ok(None)` - Process is still running\n    /// - `Err(e)` - Error occurred while checking process status\n    ///\n    /// # Example\n    ///\n    /// ```no_run\n    /// # use tests::process_guard::ProcessGuard;\n    /// # let mut guard = ProcessGuard::new(unimplemented!());\n    /// match guard.try_wait() {\n    ///     Ok(Some(status)) => println!(\"Process exited: {:?}\", status),\n    ///     Ok(None) => println!(\"Process still running\"),\n    ///     Err(e) => eprintln!(\"Error checking process: {}\", e),\n    /// }\n    /// ```\n    pub fn try_wait(&mut self) -> io::Result<Option<ExitStatus>> {\n        if let Some(ref mut child) = self.inner {\n            child.try_wait()\n        } else {\n            Ok(None)\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Kill the child process.\n    ///\n    /// Sends SIGTERM (or equivalent on non-Unix) to the child process.\n    /// Does not wait for the process to exit - call `wait()` separately to prevent zombies.\n    ///\n    /// # Returns\n    ///\n    /// - `Ok(())` - Process was killed (or was already None)\n    /// - `Err(e)` - Error occurred while killing the process\n    ///\n    /// # Example\n    ///\n    /// ```no_run\n    /// # use tests::process_guard::ProcessGuard;\n    /// # let mut guard = ProcessGuard::new(unimplemented!());\n    /// guard.kill().expect(\"failed to kill process\");\n    /// guard.wait().expect(\"failed to wait for killed process\");\n    /// ```\n    pub fn kill(&mut self) -> io::Result<()> {\n        if let Some(ref mut child) = self.inner {\n            child.kill()\n        } else {\n            Ok(())\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Wait for the child process to exit.\n    ///\n    /// Blocks until the process exits and returns its exit status.\n    ///\n    /// # Returns\n    ///\n    /// - `Ok(status)` - Process exited with the given status\n    /// - `Err(e)` - Error occurred while waiting, or no child process exists\n    ///\n    /// # Example\n    ///\n    /// ```no_run\n    /// # use tests::process_guard::ProcessGuard;\n    /// # let mut guard = ProcessGuard::new(unimplemented!());\n    /// let status = guard.wait().expect(\"failed to wait for process\");\n    /// println!(\"Process exited with status: {:?}\", status);\n    /// ```\n    pub fn wait(&mut self) -> io::Result<ExitStatus> {\n        if let Some(ref mut child) = self.inner {\n            child.wait()\n        } else {\n            Err(io::Error::other(\"No child process to wait for\"))\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for ProcessGuard {\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        // Best-effort cleanup - don't panic in drop\n        let _ = self.kill();\n        let _ = self.wait();\n        // Prevent double-wait by consuming the child after our methods handle it\n        let _ = self.inner.take();\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use std::time::Duration;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_process_guard_cleanup_on_drop() {\n        // Spawn a long-running process\n        let child = std::process::Command::new(\"sleep\")\n            .arg(\"10\")\n            .spawn()\n            .expect(\"failed to spawn sleep process\");\n\n        let pid = child.id();\n        let guard = ProcessGuard::new(child);\n\n        // Process should be running\n        assert!(process_exists(pid), \"process should be running\");\n\n        // When guard is dropped, process should be killed and cleaned up\n        drop(guard);\n        std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(100));\n\n        // Process should no longer exist\n        assert!(\n            !process_exists(pid),\n            \"process should be terminated after drop\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_process_guard_explicit_wait() {\n        // Spawn a short-lived process\n        let child = std::process::Command::new(\"true\")\n            .spawn()\n            .expect(\"failed to spawn true process\");\n\n        let mut guard = ProcessGuard::new(child);\n\n        // Wait for process to complete\n        let status = guard.wait().expect(\"failed to wait for process\");\n        assert!(status.success(), \"true should exit successfully\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_process_guard_kill() {\n        // Spawn a long-running process\n        let child = std::process::Command::new(\"sleep\")\n            .arg(\"10\")\n            .spawn()\n            .expect(\"failed to spawn sleep process\");\n\n Kill the process explicitly\n        guard.kill().expect(\"failed to kill process\");\n        guard.wait().expect(\"failed to wait for killed process\");\n\n        // Process should no longer exist\n        assert!(!process_exists(pid), \"process should be terminated\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_process_guard_try_wait() {\n        // Spawn a long-running process\n        let child = std::process::Command::new(\"sleep\")\n            .arg(\"1\")\n            .spawn()\n            .expect(\"failed to spawn sleep process\");\n\n        let mut guard = ProcessGuard::new(child);\n\n        // Process should still be running\n        match guard.try_wait() {\n            Ok(None) => {\n                // Expected - process is still running\n            }\n            Ok(Some(status)) => {\n                panic!(\n                    \"Process should still be running, but exited with: {:?}\",\n                    status\n                );\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                panic!(\"Failed to try_wait: {}\", e);\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Wait a bit and try again\n        std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(1100));\n        match guard.try_wait() {\n            Ok(Some(status)) => {\n                assert!(status.success(), \"sleep should exit successfully\");\n            }\n            Ok(None) => {\n                panic!(\"Process should have exited by now\");\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                panic!(\"Failed to try_wait: {}\", e);\n
